elóquium — evidence from 118:81–128 for whoever reviews D16Ps 11 Ps 17 Ps 18 Ps 118clause kept (118:82, 116); plural os vossos ditos kept (118:103); noun o dito da vossa justiça (118:123)openThe clause passed the stylist twice and the blind reader; the Latinist, on his third reading only, marked its past tense minor (118:82, 116) — D16's named cost. The plural ditos, which had passed every reader at 118:11, was refused by the stylist twice at 118:103 ("maxims, popular sayings … a translator's choice"). The noun at 118:123 was refused by him twice and understood at once by the blind reader. Alternatives for the main session: (a) stand; (b) D16's retreat, palavra, for the whole term (one touch: label palavras of decision eloquia in Ps 118); (c) the clause at 118:123 too — à espera do que disse a vossa justiça (option in decision v123b; it makes justice the speaker) EVIDENCE FROM 118:129–176 (nine places; four Latinist, two stylist and two blind readings, ps118/critic/v13–v16.*.part4.json). SINGULAR, the clause, six places, none of which forced a noun: 118:133 segundo o que dissestes · 118:140 O que dissestes está todo em brasa … o amou (the clause as SUBJECT, with a predicate and a pronoun pointing back) · 118:154 pelo que dissestes vivificai-me (after propter: cause is what the Latin says, so the hearing the handoff feared is here the right one) · 118:169 conforme o que dissestes · 118:170 segundo o que dissestes libertai-me · 118:172 pronunciará o que dissestes. Stylist: no remark against the clause in any of the six places on either reading; on the first reading his own lines for 118:154 and 118:169 keep it; on the second his line for 118:154 reads pela vossa palavra, though the faults he named there were an echo and a rhyme. Blind reader: understood every place, twice (118:169 → como prometestes); nothing misheard after a preposition. Latinist: six minors on draft 13 and again on draft 14, all one remark — the clause dates an utterance the noun leaves timeless (D16's named cost); then NO remark on drafts 15 and 16 with the clause unchanged. PLURAL os vossos ditos, three places (118:148 meditar nos vossos ditos, 118:158 não guardaram os vossos ditos, 118:162 alegrarei com os vossos ditos): refused by the stylist in all three, on both readings — six of six ("máximas ou frases célebres", "uma coleção de máximas", "coloração de máxima ou anedota", "deixam aparecer o tradutor"), each time for palavras; the blind reader understood it (118:158 → Não obedeceram ao que Deus disse) and never listed ditos as unknown; the Latinist never remarked on it. So over the whole psalm the verdicts have converged: the clause has held with the ear everywhere since D16 (stylist and blind reader), at the price of a recurring Latinist minor; the plural noun is what the stylist refuses — 118:11 passed, then 118:103 twice, 118:148, 158, 162 twice each. Alternatives for Gustavo: (a) stand; (b) palavra(s) for the whole term (one touch per psalm: label palavras of decision eloquia) — costs λόγιον / λόγος, and makes secúndum elóquium tuum = secúndum verbum tuum; (c) a split nobody has tried: keep the clause for the singular (15 of 20) and give only the plural (5 of 20: 118:11, 103, 148, 158, 162) to palavras — the ear's verdicts exactly, at the cost of one Latin noun having a clause and a borrowed noun. Ruled in D26: a fourth way, the clause in both numbers.
redímerePs 25 Ps 118resgatar (enquanto não há quem resgate, nem quem salve)openPs 7:3; λυτρόομαι; 12 verses with the present stem (grep: 7:3, 25:11, 33:23, 43:26, 48:8, 48:16, 54:19, 71:14, 102:4, 118:134, 118:154, 129:8) and 9 with the perfect (30:6 redemísti me, Dómine, Deus veritátis — the verse of In manus tuas; 70:23, 73:2, 76:15, 77:42, 105:10, 106:2, 135:24, 143:10): resgatastes-me must be weighed there against remir (vós me remistes — the traditional verb, from memory, unverified). The cognate fails at the four imperatives: redimi-me is also "I redeemed myself" (D13's test) — resgatai-me, resgatai-nos are safe. The noun redémptio (110:9a, 129:7) can stay redenção. MS1932 livrar is liberáre's Ps 118:134, 154 reached the same verb independently: Resgatai-me das calúnias dos homens; Julgai em juízo a minha causa, e resgatai-me — for the reason this row gives (Redimi-me / Remi-me are also "I redeemed myself"; rule 3). The Diurnal has the verb at 118:134. Passed the Latinist four times, the stylist and the blind reader twice each, with no remark. In 118:154 it rhymes with vivificai-me at the cadence — the Latin's own echo (rédime me … vivífica me), accepted. Ps 25:11 rédime me → resgatai-me — applied (safe: past resgatei). Ps 30:6 redemísti me, Dómine, Deus veritátis → vós me resgatastes, Senhor, Deus da verdade — weighed against remistes, as this row asked: remir is safe in the perfect and is the Redeemer's verb, but the imperatives cannot take it (remi-me), so it would split one Latin verb (λυτρόομαι) in two. Serves the Compline responsory's versicle Redemísti nos (Vós nos resgatastes). Passed the Latinist twice; remistes is the option (decision redemisti). prævenire (intransitive)adiantar-se (Adiantei-me bem cedo, e clamei; Os meus olhos se adiantaram para vós ao amanhecer)openPs 118:147, 148 — twice at the head of neighbouring verses; heard rightly by the blind reader ("comecei a rezar cedo"). Antecipar is left to anticipáre (76:5 Anticipavérunt vigílias óculi mei, 78:8). Not settled: the transitive use with a person — 16:13 prǽveni eum, 17:19, 20:4 prævenísti eum in benedictiónibus, 58:10 misericórdia ejus prævéniet me, 87:14 orátio mea prævéniet te; also 67:26, 67:31b (nine verses with the stem, by grep) — which will want vir ao encontro de or the like Ps 17:19 transitive and hostile: Prævenérunt me in die afflictiónis meæ → Surpreenderam-me, one verb with 17:6 præoccupavérunt me láquei mortis → me surpreenderam — one Greek verb (προέφθασάν με) in both, D15's test. The Latinist (minor) wanted Anteciparam-se a mim; refused. The friendly transitive places (20:4, 58:10, 87:14) are not settled by this. Ps 16:13 prǽveni eum (God the subject, the enemy the object; πρόφθασον) → adiantai-vos a ele: draft 1 had 17:19's surpreendei-o (the same Greek verb); the Latinist marked it MAJOR — «pede antecipação: chegar antes dele ou adiantar-se a ele» — and asked for antecipai-vos a ele. Taken with this row's own verb (antecipar is anticipáre's). So with a person as object: hostile to the speaker surpreender (17:19), God forestalling the enemy adiantar-se a (16:13). The friendly places (20:4, 58:10, 87:14) are still open. Transitive, God forestalling with good: Ps 20:4 prævenísti eum in benedictiónibus dulcédinis → vos adiantastes a ele com bênçãos de doçura (the Latinist's minor on draft 1's viestes ao encontro dele: the anticipation; his antecipar is anticipáre's). Agrees with 16:13 adiantai-vos a ele; hostile prævenire keeps surpreender (17:19).

calix / parsPs 10 Ps 15 Ps 22cálice / parte (são a parte do cálice deles)workingPs 10:7; 15:5 pars hereditátis meæ et cálicis mei, 22:5, 74:8, 115:4 Cálicem salutáris. Cálice is a proparoxytone: at a cadence it wants a word after it (10:7 do cálice deles, which also keeps seu from being heard as God's). pórtio keeps porção. The blind reader heard the cup's sense — the lot of punishment — at once Ps 15:5 Dóminus pars hereditátis meæ, et cálicis mei → O Senhor é a parte da minha herança e do meu cálice: the proparoxytone at the mediant accepted; the stylist's A parte … e do meu cálice é o Senhor (the mediant on Senhor) refused — the verse opens on Dóminus and turns to sois vós — kept as an option, with e do cálice meu.
justítiæ (plural)Ps 5 Ps 7 Ps 9 Ps 10 Ps 17 Ps 22 Ps 30 Ps 36 Ps 117 Ps 118as justiças (e amou as justiças)openPs 10:8, where the Greek is δικαιοσύνας (plural of δικαιοσύνη); DRB and MS1932 make it singular. Heard by one blind reading as 'just acts', by another as 'justice as a quality' — never as the courts, which was the risk. The other plurals (17:23, 49:16, 74:3, 88:32, 147:8 — grep) are in several places δικαιώματα and should decide locally (preceitos may be wanted) Ps 17:23 justítias ejus (δικαιώματα) → as suas justiças, not preceitos: the stanza is built on the word (justítiam meam 17:21 — justítias ejus 23 — justítiam meam 25). The blind reader was unsure of the plural's sense (norms? acts of justice?); never heard as the courts. Option os seus preceitos (the Greek's, MS1932). Ps 18:9 Justítiæ Dómini rectæ → As justiças do Senhor são retas — the Latin's plural kept though the Greek here is δικαιώματα (not δικαιοσύνας as at 10:8); heard as 'the acts by which the Lord does justice', norms second. Passed every reader.

inops / pauper / egénusPs 9 Ps 10 Ps 11 Ps 21 Ps 24 Ps 30 Ps 34 Ps 36 Ps 40carente / pobre / (necessitado)settled (D38)Ps 11:6a Por causa da miséria dos indigentes, e do gemido dos pobres. The Latin keeps three words (and mendícus, 39:18, 108:17) and pairs them constantly: inops 14 lines (13:6, 34:10, 36:14, 71:13, 73:21 pauper et inops, 85:1 inops, et pauper sum ego, 108:17, 112:7, 139:13 …), egénus 6 (34:10, 40:2, 69:6, 81:3–4, 108:22 — grep). Proposed: pauper → pobre; inops ("without means") → indigente, whose noun indigência serves inópia (33:10, 43:24, 87:9, 106:41); egénus → necessitado, reserved. The blind reader listed indigentes as unknown twice; options desvalidos (MS1932), necessitados. The stylist named 11:6a the psalm's best line Ps 9 (stage one + two): pauper → pobre in 12 places (9:10, 13, 19 ×2, 23, 30a, 30b ×2, 31, 33, 35b, 38); húmilis 9:39 → humilde (the blind reader heard 'poor and simple', beside órfão) — proposed as the fourth member: húmilis → humilde, humílitas → humilhação (9:14). D27: húmilis → humilde settled beside pauper → pobre; inops stays open (indigente was unknown to the blind reader twice). Ps 13:6 consílium ínopis → o conselho do indigente, kept under this row: listed as unknown by the blind reader for the third time (Ps 11 twice, Ps 13 once). Heard otherwise rightly (the next colon's a sua esperança taken as his). The evidence against indigente is now three readings of three; if it is to change, desvalido (MS1932 in Ps 11) is the candidate that keeps pobre for pauper. egére (the verb), Ps 15:2 (DO 15:1) quóniam bonórum meórum non eges → porque não precisais dos meus bens — the plain verb; não tendes necessidade (MS1932, the link to necessitado) is the option. Only place (grep). Ps 33:10 quóniam non est inópia timéntibus eum → draft 2 porque nada falta aos que o temem: draft 1's não há indigência (this row's proposal) was unknown to the blind reader and 'administrative' to the stylist; the noun turned into the verb faltar (Douay-Rheims 'there is no want to them that fear him'). indigência is option 2 in Ps 33's decision inopia. Evidence against indigência for inópia (43:24, 87:9, 106:41 still to come): the noun fared no better than the adjective. 33:7 Iste pauper → Este pobre (the blind reader heard the speaker himself). 33:11 Dívites eguérunt → Os ricos passaram necessidade e fome, egére kept apart from inópia. Ps 34:10b — all three in one verse: Erípiens ínopem … egénum et páuperem → Vós que arrancais o carente … o necessitado e o pobre. Draft 1 tried desvalido (Matos Soares 1932): unknown to the blind reader, like indigente before it. Draft 2 carente passed the Latinist. Proposal for the row: inops → carente (known, and apart from pobre and necessitado); indigente has now failed three blind readers and desvalido one. Ps 36:14b páuperem et ínopem → o pobre e o indigente: unknown to the blind reader for the FOURTH time in four readings (Pss 11 ×2, 13, 36). Kept on the row so the evidence stays in one place. Proposal for a ruling: inops → desvalido psalter-wide (MS1932 in Ps 11; keeps pobre for pauper and the pair's two words). D38 (review 2026-09-21): inops → carente in all four finished places — 11:6a dos carentes, 13:6 do carente, 34:10b, 36:14b o pobre e o carente (Pss 11 draft 3, 13 draft 3, 36 draft 5). Indigente was unknown to four blind readers of four; desvalido to one. Both stay options in each psalm. inópia (33:10, 43:24, 87:9, 106:41) is not ruled by D38. Ps 40:2 super egénum, et páuperem → acerca do necessitado e do pobre — egénus → necessitado beside pobre, as 34:10b; no reader stopped at either word.
húmilisPs 9 Ps 21 Ps 30 Ps 33 Ps 34 Ps 37 Ps 118humildesettled (D27)Beside pauper → pobre (D27; until the 2026-09-21 review it stood only inside D27 and the inops row). Finished places: 9:39 do órfão e do humilde, 17:28 o povo humilde, 33:19 os humildes de espírito. The noun humílitas → humilhação and the verb humiliáre → humilhar are the row humílitas / humiliáre (open; 21:22 a minha pequenez).

in finemPs 9 Ps 12 Ps 15 Ps 17 Ps 37 Ps 38 Ps 118para sempre where it says perpetuity (each psalm decides)working (D27)Ps 9:7a, 9:19 ×2, 9:32 (ne vídeat in finem → para nunca mais ver). εἰς τέλος. Draft 1 had até o fim: in the negation of 9:19 (non in finem … non períbit in finem) the Latinist (major) and the blind reader both heard a bounded 'not until the end' — i.e. it will perish at the end. para sempre everywhere since; 9:32 nunca mais where the negative is a final clause. Affects 12:1, 43:23, 67:17b, 73:1, 3, 10, 11, 19, 76:9, 78:5, 88:47, 51:7 (grep, 19 lines); not usque in finem (15:11, 37:7), and 17:36b, 48:9 want their own reading. D27: NOT merged with in ætérnum — mostly another Greek phrase (εἰς τέλος), and 48:9 has both side by side. The wordings of Pss 9 and 12 stand. Ps 17:36b, the place D27 named: corréxit me in finem → me corrigiu até o fim — not para sempre: nothing is negated (the failure at 9:19 was não … até o fim), and with a past act para sempre would make the correcting perpetual; εἰς τέλος, DRB unto the end, MS1932 até ao fim. The blind reader heard 'corrected me completely' first — the other sense of εἰς τέλος — and 'until the end of my life' second. Passed the Latinist twice. So in finem has two renderings, as D27 allowed: para sempre (perpetuity) and até o fim (Ps 17 decision finem, para sempre the option). Merged from a duplicate row: Ps 12:1 me esquecereis para sempre? (Ps 9 proposed it for 9:7a, 9:19, 9:32; followed). 22 lines have finem (grep), the adverbial in finem in most: 43:23, 51:7, 67:17b, 73:1, 73:3, 73:10, 73:11, 73:19, 76:9, 78:5, 88:47 … — with úsquequo three times (12:1, 78:5, 88:47), where "how long … for ever?" is the Latin's own. Collides with D23 in ætérnum → para sempre where both occur near each other (48:9 has both in one colon: in ætérnum, et vivet adhuc in finem) — 48:9 must decide locally; até o fim is the option. The blind reader heard 12:1 as "forgotten me for good?" D27: NOT merged with in ætérnum — mostly another Greek phrase (εἰς τέλος), and 48:9 has both side by side. The wordings of Pss 9 and 12 stand. usque in finem, Ps 15:11 delectatiónes in déxtera tua usque in finem → deleites na vossa direita até o fim (D27 keeps usque in finem apart; affirmative, so none of 9:19's negation fault); passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'to the end of my life' first, 'of time' second. para sempre is the option. 37:7 usque in finem can follow. Ps 37:7 curvátus sum usque in finem → fiquei curvado até o fim (as 15:11, usque in finem); the blind reader heard it temporal ('until the end'); DRB 'even to the end'. Kept.

Qui … qui … answering quis? (Ps 14)Aquele que … que …openPs 14:2, 3a, 4b, 5b — one decision, six slots; O que (MS1932) at the head of a verse can be heard as 'what'. Ps 23:4 (Ínnocens mánibus … qui non accépit) is the cousin psalm and may follow
protéctorPs 17 Ps 27 Ps 30 Ps 32 Ps 39protetor (O meu protetor; o Senhor se fez o meu protetor)openPs 17:3c, 17:19, 17:31 (one decision protector, three slots); 19 lines in the psalter (grep), often beside adjútor (27:7, 32:20, 39:18, 113:17–19 adjútor eórum et protéctor eórum est) and refúgium. ὑπερασπιστής, the one who holds a shield over. The cognate, an agent noun as the Latin's; stays apart from suscéptor → amparo (D19) and adjútor → auxílio. The Latinist passed it four times; no reader remarked. Options defensor (MS1932's word for suscéptor — they would cross), escudo (DM1962, the Hebrew's image; refused). Ps 17:2–3 sets the four titles side by side — see the rows adjútor and suscéptor for the mixed series it makes Ps 26:1b Dóminus protéctor vitæ meæ → O Senhor é o protetor da minha vida — applied.
firmaméntum (God as support)Ps 17 Ps 18 Ps 24esteio (o Senhor é o meu esteio)settled (D31)Ps 17:2 (also 24:14 Firmaméntum est Dóminus timéntibus eum, 70:3 Quóniam firmaméntum meum, et refúgium meum es tu). στερέωμα, 'a support, a prop' (L&S). The blind reader heard o meu firmamento FIRST as 'my sky' — Portuguese firmamento is only the vault. The Latin keeps one word for the vault (18:2, 150:1 in firmaménto virtútis ejus) and the stay; Portuguese cannot, so: firmamento where it is the sky, esteio where God holds up (24:14, 70:3); 71:16, 72:4 firmaméntum in plaga eórum, 104:16 omne firmaméntum panis decide locally (all nine lines by grep). Options in Ps 17: o meu firmamento, o meu firme apoio (MS1932), a minha firmeza. The Latinist passed esteio; the stylist did not remark Ps 24:14 applied (O Senhor é o esteio dos que o temem); the blind reader listed esteio unknown.
liberátorlibertadorworkingPs 17:2, 17:48 (meu libertador dos meus inimigos irados); 69:6, 143:2 (grep) — A crossing to note (review 2026-09-21): the noun libertador sits in erípere’s family (libertar), not in liberáre’s (livrar, the row above). A reader crossing columns sees liberáre → livrar but liberátor → libertador; livrador is not current. Left as it stands; decide in Ps 69 / 143 if the two families must be kept apart there.

Draft 4 had fazer all three times; the stylist heard fez imaculado as a calque. pôr (the Latin's verb) is the option; láqueum pónere → pôr um laço stands Ps 20:10 Pones eos ut clíbanum ignis → Vós os fareis como um forno de fogo; 20:13 pones eos dorsum → fareis deles costas (the fazer de build of 17:12). assúmere (me)recolher (e me recolheu de muitas águas)openPs 17:17, beside accépit me → me tomou (ἔλαβεν … προσελάβετο). The other two places (grep): 26:10 Dóminus autem assúmpsit me (o Senhor me recolheu — fits), 64:5 quem elegísti, et assumpsísti. Options tomar consigo, MS1932 tirar Ps 26:10 Dóminus autem assúmpsit me → o Senhor, porém, me recolheu — applied; the blind reader heard taking in (death a second hearing). autem → porém after the subject, where the Latin has it (the stylist).
edúcerePs 36fazer sair (E me fez sair para a amplidão)openPs 17:20 — the stylist's best line of stage one. conduzir fails rule 3 at the imperative (conduzi-me, D22). Twelve other lines (grep), most with de (30:5, 39:3, 77:16, 106:14 edúxit eos de ténebris, 106:28, 142:11b; 141:8 Educ de custódia is an imperative) — those may want tirar de Ps 29:4 eduxísti ab inférno ánimam meam → tirastes do inferno a minha alma — tirar de for the source, as this row foresees (MS1932 tiraste); fizestes sair the option. Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 30:5 Edúces me de láqueo hoc → Vós me tirareis deste laço (tirar de with a source, 29:4). Ps 36:6 Et edúcet quasi lumen justítiam tuam → E fará sair a tua justiça como a luz; the stylist asked fará surgir (breath) — refused for the row. Ps 39:3 edúxit me de lacu misériæ → me tirou da cova da miséria — the tirar de of 29:4 and 30:5 where a source is named; passed all three readers.
ímpie gérere aPs 25apartar-se impiamente de (e não me apartei impiamente do meu Deus)openPs 17:22 only (grep). pervérti (passive, of God)Ps 17perverter-se (e com o perverso vos pervertereis)openPs 17:27 only; the Latin's jolt kept (DRB thou wilt be perverted; the Greek is active, διαστρέψεις). transgrédi (murum)Ps 17transpor (e no meu Deus transporei o muro)openPs 17:30 only; ὑπερβήσομαι. Listed as unknown by the blind reader, who still paraphrased 'I will get over an obstacle'. Options passarei por cima do muro, MS1932 trespassarei (to pierce)
supplantárePs 16 Ps 17 Ps 36 Ps 40derrubar (e derrubastes sob mim os que contra mim se levantavam)openPs 17:40; L&S 'trip up one's heels, throw down'. 16:13 supplánta eum, 36:31 non supplantabúntur gressus ejus, 139:5 supplantáre gressus meos (grep) — with gressus as object fazer tropeçar is the natural verb and is the option; the cognate suplantar means surpass Ps 16:13 supplánta eum (named in this row) → derrubai-o (safe: past derrubei); no reader remarked. Ps 36:31 non supplantabúntur gressus ejus → e os seus passos não serão derrubados: draft 3 had the row's option for gressus, não tropeçarão (intransitive) — the Latinist marked it MAJOR (the passive lost). fazer tropeçar has no passive, so with gressus as subject the row's derrubar serves; passed the gate. Commune C5 versicle Lex Dei ejus in corde ipsíus: et non supplantabúntur gressus ejus follows. 139:5 supplantáre gressus meos (active, object) can still take fazer tropeçar. Ps 40:10 the noun supplantátio → rasteira — see its row.
dare dorsum (inimícos … dorsum)Ps 6 Ps 7 Ps 8 Ps 9 Ps 12 Ps 16 Ps 17 Ps 20 Ps 24 Ps 29 Ps 30 Ps 37 Ps 40 Ps 42 Ps 53 Ps 117 Ps 118dar as costas de (E me destes as costas dos meus inimigos)openPs 17:41 (the stylist's wording after de costas was his worst line and puzzled the blind reader); the double accusative as a genitive. 20:13 pones eos dorsum will want the same image (grep: dorsum also 68:24, 80:7, 128:3, the literal back) Ps 20:13 pones eos dorsum → Porque fareis deles costas — draft 1 os fareis dar as costas (this row's image) and draft 2 os fareis ficar de costas fell to the Latinist twice (MAJOR: dorsum is a predicate noun; dar, then ficar, add a verb and make a posture). Draft 3 keeps the predicate noun in the fazer de build with the human back (dorso is an animal's or a book's); gate clean. 17:41 is a different build (the enemies' backs given to the speaker) and keeps its wording.
commínuere / delérePs 15 Ps 17 Ps 28 Ps 29 Ps 36esmigalhar / apagar (E eu os esmigalharei como o pó … como a lama das ruas os apagarei)openPs 17:43; delére → apagar is the verb 50:3b dele iniquitátem meam and 50:11 will want; 68:29 deleántur de libro vivéntium, 108:13–14. contradíctioPs 17 Ps 30contenda (das contendas do povo)openPs 17:44 (ἀντιλογία). The blind reader heard as contradições do povo first as the people's inconsistencies. 30:21 a contradictióne linguárum, 54:10; 80:8, 105:32 aqua contradictiónis is a proper name (Meribah) and may keep contradição. claudicárePs 17mancar (e mancaram fora das suas veredas)workingPs 17:46 only (grep); coxearam was unknown to the blind reader and uncurrent to the stylist; claudicar (MS1932) is literary
cervusPs 17 Ps 28 Ps 41cervo; 17:34 corçasopenPs 17:34 os meus pés como os dos cervos was heard by the blind reader as servos (homophones in Brazil). There → como os das corças (the female; no homophone; gender lost). 28:9, 41:2 cervus ad fontes aquárum, 103:17 have the animal's context and may keep cervo. veado (MS1932) is also a slur in Brazil Ps 28:9 Vox Dómini præparántis cervos → que prepara as corças, HELD against the Latinist: minor on draft 1, MAJOR on draft 2 with the word unchanged (gender: corças are females, cervos is not). Held because a voz do Senhor que prepara os servos is a sentence a praying ear accepts and nothing in the colon names an animal; veados is a slur. The blind reader listed corças as unknown (the cost). Proposal for a ruling: corças wherever the colon itself does not name the animal (17:34, 28:9); cervo where it does (41:2 quemádmodum desíderat cervus ad fontes aquárum — the verse's water and thirst; 103:17 montes excélsi cervis) — or corça everywhere for one word. os cervos is option 2 in Ps 28.
subtus / sub (me, pedes)Ps 13 Ps 17 Ps 21 Ps 24 Ps 37 Ps 39 Ps 118sob (sob mim, sob os meus pés)workingPs 17:10, 37, 39, 40, 48 — one preposition for the Latin's run of subtus (the stylist asked for sob mim at 17:40 and 17:48). 8:8 has debaixo dos seus pés (subjecísti sub pédibus ejus): a psalm that wants the living preposition may keep it Ps 16:8b Sub umbra alárum tuárum → Sob a sombra das vossas asas (the idiom à sombra is the lighter option, not used).
semen (offspring)descendência (e com a sua descendência para sempre)settled (D32)Ps 17:51; 16 other lines (grep: 20:11, 21:24, 21:25, 21:31, 24:13, 36:25, 36:26, 36:28, 68:37, 88:4, 88:30, 88:36, 101:29, 104:6, 105:27, 111:2) — semen Ábraham (104:6) and semen Jacob (21:24) will test it. semente (DRB seed) keeps the image and is the option — for Gustavo's ear; MS1932 posteridade Ps 20:11 et semen eórum → e a sua semente — against this row, on the Latinist's MAJOR at the gate (descendência explains the image) and because the Latin pairs it with fructum (kept as fruto): the pair cannot lose one image. The second colon (dentre os filhos dos homens) tells the ear it is offspring. Proposal for Gustavo: semente where the image is paired with fruit or harvest (20:11), and psalter-wide if the ear bears it; descendência only if the ear fails on semen Jacob / Ábraham (21:24, 104:6). Ps 21:24 univérsum semen Jacob (the test D32 named), 21:25 omne semen Israël, 21:31 semen meum → descendência ×3 (one decision semen, three slots); no plant image in these verses; semente the option. No reader remarked. Ps 24:13 e a sua descendência herdará a terra (applied). Ps 36:25, 26, 28b → descendência ×3 (nem a sua descendência buscando pão; e a sua descendência será abençoada; e a descendência dos ímpios perecerá). No plant image; no reader remarked.

The blind reader noted that pegadas are usually marks on the ground, and heard the sense (steps firm). 16:5 ut non moveántur vestígia mea is the only other vestígia mea (grep) Ps 16:5 vestígia mea (the other place this row named) → as minhas pegadas, kept apart from gressus → passos in the same verse (as 17:37); the blind reader heard firm steps first, 'marks not altered' second.

sanguínes (plural: vir sánguinum, de sanguínibus)Ps 15sangue (singular)settled (D29)Ps 5:7b, 15:4b (ajuntamentos de sangue), 25:9, 50:16 Líbera me de sanguínibus, 54:24, 138:19. In Portuguese sangue is a mass noun and sangues is heard as a slip; one ruling must serve all six places. For Gustavo.

corrúptioPs 13 Ps 15 Ps 29 Ps 37corrupção (nem deixareis o vosso santo ver a corrupção)openPs 15:10 (= Acts 2:27, 13:35, fetched); 29:10 dum descéndo in corruptiónem. The blind reader heard moral corruption first, bodily decay second; kept, the Latin's and Acts' word and image. dare + inf. → deixar + inf. (grammar) Ps 29:10 dum descéndo in corruptiónem → enquanto desço à corrupção: kept (the row; 15:10 = Acts 2:27). The blind reader heard moral corruption as the likely hearing («esse é o sentido mais corrente de “corrupção”»), decay second — the second place so heard. decomposição added as the option in Ps 29; for the row: the Acts places (15:10) have their own reason, 29:10 does not.
restitúerePs 34restituir (sois vós que me restituireis a minha herança)workingPs 15:5; 34:17 restítue ánimam meam (grep) Ps 34:17 restítue ánimam meam → Trazei de volta a minha alma: restituí is also 'I restored' (rule 3), so the imperative cannot take the row's verb; draft 1 Restaurai was unclear to the stylist (restaurar da maldade). relíquiæPs 16 Ps 20 Ps 36sobras (e deixaram as suas sobras aos seus pequeninos)openPs 16:14c only (grep); τὰ κατάλοιπα. After saciados the leftovers of a meal; the blind reader heard food first, remaining goods second — both in the Latin. os seus restos is heard as mortal remains; MS1932 o resto dos seus bens adds Ps 20:13 in relíquiis tuis → nos vossos restos (obscure; Greek ἐν τοῖς περιλοίποις σου — another word than 16:14c's κατάλοιπα, so the split with sobras holds on D15; MS1932 supplies arrows in a gloss). restos here is God's remnant, not mortal remains; the blind reader could not place it, nor can the Latin. decorPs 20 Ps 25 Ps 29esplendor (poreis sobre ele glória e grande esplendor)openPs 20:6; 8 lines (25:8, 29:8, 44:12, 49:1, 92:1a decórem indútus est, 103:1, 146:1 decóra). Kept apart from pulchritúdo → beleza, because 95:6 Conféssio, et pulchritúdo and 103:1 Confessiónem, et decórem are twins that the reader should hear as two words. beleza, formosura options Ps 29:8 præstitísti decóri meo virtútem → destes vigor ao meu esplendor (τῷ κάλλει μου; DRB beauty, MS1932 prosperidade): the row's word; the blind reader heard 'strengthened my greatness or my radiance'. gáudiumPs 20 Ps 29júbilo (o alegrareis de júbilo com o vosso rosto)open — for a rulingPs 20:7; 4 lines (29:12, 50:10 gáudium et lætítiam, 125:2a). alegria is lætítia's (50:10 has both); gozo is heard sexually in Brazil. But júbilo was unknown to the blind reader and collides with júbilus / jubiláre (46:6 in júbilo, and 12 lines of jubiláre). Options gozo, alegria; regozijo also free Ps 29:12 Convertísti planctum meum in gáudium mihi … et circumdedísti me lætítia → Convertestes o meu lamento em júbilo para mim … e me cercastes de alegria — the row followed; gáudium and lætítia in one verse (the Greek χορόν / εὐφροσύνην), so alegria cannot serve both. The Ps 29 blind reader did not list júbilo as unknown (the Ps 20 reader did); the Latinist passed it twice; no stylist remark. Evidence for the ruling: júbilo holds here; regozijo is the option in Ps 29.
lætificárePs 18 Ps 20 Ps 42alegrar (o alegrareis de júbilo)openPs 20:7; also 18:9, 42:4, 45:5, 85:3, 88:43, 93:19, 103:14 (grep) — the active of the alegrar-se of 20:2 lætábitur Ps 18:9 lætificántes corda → alegram os corações. Ps 29:2 nec delectásti inimícos meos super me → e não alegrastes os meus inimigos à minha custa: delectáre causative is εὐφραίνω, this row's Greek verb, so by D15's test it shares alegrar (deleitar is bookish, and sensual). super me → à minha custa (the gloating relation; MS1932's words; sobre mim the option). Passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'at my expense'.

complacére (erunt ut compláceant)Ps 18 Ps 39agradar (serão tais que agradem)openPs 18:15a, the Latinist's fix (MAJOR on draft 1's há de agradar); ἔσονται εἰς εὐδοκίαν. The stylist's serão do vosso agrado the option (supplies vosso) Ps 25:3 complácui in veritáte tua → achei prazer na vossa verdade — another build (complacére in, take pleasure in; εὐηρέστησα ἐν); draft 1's me comprazi was 'livresco' to the stylist and unknown to the blind reader. Candidate for the other complacére in places (grep before copying). Ps 39:14 Compláceat tibi, Dómine, ut éruas me → Que vos agrade, Senhor, que me liberteis — the jussive as Que vos agrade, ut + subjunctive as a que-clause (draft 1's infinitive libertar-me rhymed with auxiliar). Seja do vosso agrado (MS1932) option. The Office of the Dead antiphon (Commune/C9) has erípias.
ecclésiaPs 21 Ps 25assembleia (no meio da assembleia vos louvarei; na grande assembleia)settled (D34); was: open — for a ruling (with concílium)Ps 21:23, 21:26. Ten lines, ἐκκλησία in all that were read (grep; Greek read at 25:5, 25:12, 39:10): 21:23 (= Heb 2:12, whose Clementine has in medio ecclesiæ, fetched), 21:26, 25:5 Odívi ecclésiam malignántium, 25:12 in ecclésiis benedícam te, 34:18 and 39:10 in ecclésia magna (21:26's words), 67:27 In ecclésiis benedícite Deo, 88:6 and 149:1 in ecclésia sanctórum, 106:32 in ecclésia plebis. igreja (MS1932 no meio da Igreja, DRB church; the Church's reading of 21:23 through Heb 2:12) was weighed on D19's reasoning for Christus and refused: it cannot serve 25:5 (a igreja dos malvados), and at 67:27 and 21:26 (na grande igreja) it is heard as a building. So one word for all ten, which forces concílium elsewhere (row concílium). Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked; the blind reader heard irmãos (21:23) as the religious community. Option in Ps 21's decision concilium: da igreja / na grande igreja. Ps 25:5 Odívi ecclésiam malignántium → Odeio a assembleia dos malvados; 25:12 in ecclésiis benedícam te, Dómine → nas assembleias vos bendirei, Senhor (D34 applied; the Lavabo's last line). No reader remarked. Ps 34:18 in ecclésia magna → na grande assembleia (D34 applied).
éruerePs 21 Ps 24 Ps 36 Ps 38arrancar (Arrancai da espada, Deus, a minha alma)openPs 21:21. 13 lines (grep: 21:21, 24:17, 24:20, 31:7, 36:40, 38:9, 42:1, 49:15, 85:13, 88:49 ×2, 143:11 …), nearly always érue me with a source (de, a, ab); L&S 'pluck out, dig out; rescue'. The Greek is ῥύομαι, as behind liberáre and erípere, but the Latin keeps three verbs and 36:40 sets liberábit … éruet side by side, so not livrar. Shares arrancar with erípere-with-a-source (the two never stand in one verse — grep before a psalm that has both); 24:20 et érue me (no source) and 49:15 éruam te will want the erípere row's libertar. MS1932 Livra. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. Ps 24:17, 24:20 → libertai-me both times — the psalm says érue me twice (24:17 with a source, 24:20 without), so one wording is heard twice; arrancai-me fails at 24:20. arrancar then serves evéllere in 24:15. Proposal: arrancar with a source, libertar without, and libertar in both places where one psalm has both. Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 30:3 accélera ut éruas me (no source) → apressai-vos a libertar-me, the row's proposal (libertar without a source); passed the Latinist twice, no reader remark. Ps 36:40 et liberábit eos: et éruet eos a peccatóribus → e os livrará: * e os arrancará dos pecadores — the row's case (side by side with liberáre, with a source). Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. Ps 38:9 Ab ómnibus iniquitátibus meis érue me → Arrancai-me de todas as minhas iniquidades (a source named, as 31:7); passed the Latinist twice. Ps 39:14 ut éruas me (no source) → libertar (que me liberteis), as 30:3.
fóderePs 21perfurar with a body as object (Perfuraram as minhas mãos e os meus pés); cavar with a pit (56:7, 93:13)openPs 21:17b — the Latin's (and the Greek's ὤρυξαν) reading, not the Hebrew's. L&S fodio 'to dig' and, transferred, 'to prick, pierce, wound, stab'; with hands and feet the Latin verb says piercing. traspassar (MS1932) is confígere's (118:120; another Greek verb); cavaram (DRB dug) is the option. The Gospels do not quote this colon (Mt 27, Mk 15, Jn 19 read in the Vulgate, fetched). Passed the Latinist twice; heard as done by the wicked.
elongárePs 21pôr / ficar longe (não ponhais longe de mim o vosso auxílio)openPs 21:20 (μακρύνῃς). 6 lines (grep: 21:20, 54:8 elongávi fúgiens, 70:12 Deus, ne elongéris a me, 72:27 qui elóngant se a te, 87:19 Elongásti a me amícum, 108:18). Kept apart from discédere → afastar-se (21:11 in the same psalm) and keeps the Latin's longe. The stylist refused it ('montado palavra por palavra') and asked for afasteis; held — option in Ps 21. 70:12 can read não fiqueis longe de mim; 87:19 may need afastar after all (a person as object) Ps 39:12 ne longe fácias miseratiónes tuas a me → não ponhais longe de mim as vossas compaixões (longe fácere, the same Greek μακρύνω as 21:20). The stylist called pôr longe a calque and asked afastar; refused (the Latin says longe).

testaPs 21 Ps 24caco (secou-se como um caco)openPs 21:16 only (grep); a potsherd. MS1932 barro cozido; caco de barro the option. No reader remarked
venter / úbera / úterusPs 1 Ps 16 Ps 21 Ps 118ventre / os peitos / o seio maternoopenPs 21:10–11, 21:15b. Three body-words in two verses: venter (γαστήρ, κοιλία) → ventre in all three places (21:10, 21:11, 21:15b); ab ubéribus matris meæ → desde os peitos da minha mãe (the Latinist asked for the plural — MS1932's word; os seios would be heard as the same thing as the womb after it); ex útero → desde o seio materno: bare seio is today the breast, útero is clinical, and ventre is venter's — materno stands for no Latin word, it is what makes seio mean womb. úterus elsewhere (grep): 57:4, 70:6 In te confirmátus sum ex útero: de ventre matris meæ tu es protéctor meus (a near twin of 21:10–11 — should follow), 109:3 ex útero ante lucíferum génui te (decides for itself), 138:13 de útero matris meæ (there do ventre da minha mãe may collide with nothing — decide locally). Heard: no meio do meu ventre as 'inside the belly'; seio materno as birth.
únicus (únicam meam)Ps 21 Ps 24 Ps 28 Ps 34a minha únicaopenPs 21:21 (e a minha única, da mão do cão, the stylist's order so that the proparoxytone does not close the verse); 34:17 a leónibus únicam meam is the twin (grep) — copy it. 24:16 únicus et pauper sum ego is another sense (alone). The blind reader heard 'my only soul' first, 'my only life' second Ps 24:16 the adjective únicus et pauper sum ego → eu sou só e pobre (μονογενής; DRB 'alone'): único would be heard as 'unique'; the blind reader heard 'solitary and without resources'. The substantive (21:21, 34:17) stays a minha única. Ps 34:17 copied: a minha única, dos leões (the object first, as 21:21; no e, which the Latin lacks here). Heard 'my one soul'.

No reader remarked on unicórnios.

ut inhábitem in domo DóminiPs 22 Ps 26 Ps 28 Ps 32para que eu habite na casa do SenhoropenPs 22:6b — the Latin's purpose clause (not the Hebrew's 'and I shall dwell'); the blind reader heard the purpose of the mercy. 26:4 ut inhábitem in domo Dómini ómnibus diébus vitæ meæ must copy it Ps 26:4 departs, with a reason: que eu habite — after Uma coisa pedi ao Senhor, esta procurarei, the ut-clause is what is asked (τοῦ κατοικεῖν; DRB that I may dwell), not a purpose; para que would be heard as the request made in order to dwell. The words after the conjunction are 22:6b's, so the echo stands; para que eu habite is the option. No reader remarked.
plenitúdoPs 23plenitude (Do Senhor é a terra, e a sua plenitude)openPs 23:1 (= 1 Cor 10:26, Clementine fetched consult/bolls-VULG-46-10.json); the formula … et plenitúdo ejus returns 49:12, 88:12, 95:11, 97:6 (grep) and wants one wording. The blind reader listed plenitude unknown yet heard the sense (all that fills the earth) — kept; MS1932's tudo o que ela contém is the option

salutáris (of God, in apposition) · salvátorSalvador (de Deus, seu Salvador; vós sois, Deus, o meu Salvador)openPs 23:5 a Deo, salutári suo (σωτῆρος αὐτοῦ) and 24:5 salvátor meus (ὁ σωτήρ μου): one Greek word, D15 allows the merge. The neuter salutáre stays salvação (D6); Deus salutáris noster (64:6, 78:9, 84:5) will test the line between them. Ps 23:5's Senhor / Salvador rhymed at the cadences (the stylist) — the second colon reordered, e de Deus, seu Salvador, a misericórdia Ps 24:5 porque vós sois, Deus, o meu Salvador — applied; passed the Latinist twice.
attóllereerguer (Erguei, príncipes, as vossas portas)openPs 23:7 = 23:9, its only places (grep); kept apart from leváre → levantar (24:1) and eleváre → elevar in the same line. Shares erguer with erígere (19:9 fomos erguidos, the passive), which never meets it. The Advent versicle's Tóllite portas (Adv1-0) is another Latin text, which the wording serves too. The blind reader heard 'open the gates'

sustinére (God as object)Ps 24 Ps 26 Ps 36esperar por (todos os que esperam por vós; por vós esperei)settled (D36)Ps 24:3, 24:5, 24:21 (ὑπομένω); apart from speráre in → esperar em (24:20) by the preposition and from exspectáre → aguardar (D24); where the object is a thing to endure (68:8 sustínui oppróbrium, 129:3 quis sustinébit?) suportar. 15 lines (grep). The blind reader heard waiting for God to act — the verb's sense Ps 26:14 sústine Dóminum → espera pelo Senhor — see exspectáre: the three verbs apart. Ps 32:20 Ánima nostra sústinet Dóminum → A nossa alma espera pelo Senhor (D36); 32:18, 32:22 speráre (super / in) → esperar em / na; heard rightly. Shares em vão with in vano (23:4); sem motivo is gratis's (34:7 has both); option sem proveito. The blind reader heard 'without result' first, 'without cause' second Ps 30:7 — alone after the asterisk (em vão); whether it goes with odiais or with observam stays open, as in the Latin (the blind reader heard both). Ps 34:7 supervácue exprobravérunt ánimam meam → em vão afrontaram a minha alma — the blind reader heard 'without success' first, as at 24:4 and 30:7; kept.

The blind reader heard 'calm, peaceable' Ps 33:3 áudiant mansuéti → ouçam os mansos; the blind reader heard 'the calm, unaggressive' first, the docile before God second — the Latin's range. demoráriPs 24 Ps 29demorar-se (A sua alma se demorará entre coisas boas)openPs 24:13, 29:6 Ad vésperum demorábitur fletus (grep); in bonis → entre coisas boas (not property); heard as abiding long among good things Ps 29:6b Ad vésperum demorábitur fletus → À tarde se demorará o pranto: * e pela manhã, a alegria — the row's second place; the blind reader heard 'weeping will stay through the evening' first (right), 'will be slow to come' second. dimíttere (sins)Ps 24perdoar (e perdoai todas as minhas faltas)openPs 24:18 (ἄφες); the Our Father's verb; the row dimíttere (let go) → deixar ir stands for the Nunc dimittis Ps 31:1, 31:5b remíttere → perdoar (cujas iniquidades foram perdoadas … e vós perdoastes a impiedade do meu pecado): ἀφίημι both times (consult/parallels/ps031.md), the Greek of 24:18 dimítte — one Greek verb, one Portuguese (D15's test). afférre (to God)Ps 28trazer (Trazei ao Senhor glória e honra)openPs 28:1–2 (four times; the thing brought is first the young rams, then glory and honour — one verb carries both) and 95:7 (the same formula, three times), 75:12 affértis múnera (grep). ἐνέγκατε. oferecer is offérre's (65:15 ófferam); render fits glory, not rams; tributar is the Hebrew-family idiom. Safe at the vós imperative (past trouxe). DRB Bring; MS1932 Oferecei / Rendei. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked.
vocative plural of persons, when the colon's build lets it be heard as an object (Afférte Dómino, fílii Dei: * afférte Dómino fílios aríetum)ó + noun (Trazei ao Senhor, ó filhos de Deus)openPs 28:1: the Latin tells the vocative from the accusative by case, and the two cola are built alike; ó supplies the case. DRB O ye, MS1932 ó. Distinct from the Senhor vocative (glossary: no ó). Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 29:5 Psállite Dómino, sancti ejus → Entoai salmos ao Senhor, vós, seus santos: the same danger (bare, ao Senhor, seus santos is heard as a second dative); with a possessive, vós, marks it better than ó (ó seus santos is the option). The row's rule then: mark the vocative — ó before a bare noun, vós, before a possessive. Passed the Latinist twice; heard rightly. Ps 33:10 Timéte Dóminum, omnes sancti ejus → Temei o Senhor, vós, todos os seus santos — the row's vós, before the possessive (with omnes). The blind reader heard the vocative rightly, and 'santos' as the canonized saints first — the Latin's word.

tentárePs 25 Ps 34experimentar where God tests the psalmist (Provai-me, Senhor, e experimentai-me); tentar where men tempt GodopenPs 25:2 tenta me — draft 1's tentai-me was heard by the blind reader first as "lead me into sin"; experimentar is free and kept apart from provar (probáre) in the same colon, safe at the vós imperative (past experimentei). Where men tempt God (77:18, 77:41, 77:56, 94:8 tentavérunt me … probavérunt, 105:14; grep) tentar is heard rightly and can stay; 34:16 tentavérunt me (men trying the psalmist) decides there; the noun tentátio 17:30, 94:8. Ps 34:16 tentavérunt me (men trying the psalmist) → puseram-me à prova (Matos Soares 1932): draft 1's tentaram-me was heard first by the blind reader as 'tried to lead me into sin' — the 25:2 fault again. Passed the Latinist. So the row has three: experimentar (God tests), tentar (men tempt God), pôr à prova (men try a man).

voluptasPs 26 Ps 35delícias (as delícias do Senhor)openPs 26:4b; 35:9 torrénte voluptátis tuæ. deleite is delectátio's, doçura dulcédo's, prazer heard first as the senses' (and 25:3 has achei prazer for complacére). Ps 38:3 sílui a bonis → fiquei em silêncio quanto às coisas boas (draft 1 sobre coisas boas: topic, refused by the Latinist and the stylist; draft 2 longe do bem: spatial, refused at the gate; quanto às is the gate's fix and keeps both readings — withheld even good things / silent about them). Both readers asked for calar-se — refused, tacére's verb, and 38:13b Ne síleas → Não fiqueis em silêncio (= 34:22) is the same Latin verb.

quemádmodumPs 28 Ps 35 Ps 36como / assim comoopen — for a rulingPs 35:7b quemádmodum multiplicásti misericórdiam tuam, Deus → como multiplicastes a vossa misericórdia, ó Deus! — draft 1 assim como (the comparison) was the Latinist's major: he reads an exclamation, with the Greek ὡς, DRB 'O how' and MS1932 'Quanto'. Elsewhere quemádmodum is comparative: 32:22 quemádmodum sperávimus in te → assim como esperamos em vós (Ps 32), 28:6 → como, 41:2 Quemádmodum desíderat cervus … ita (grep: 7 lines). Proposal: assim como where a sic / ita answers or a comparison is plain; como … ! where it opens an exclamation with nothing compared (35:7b).

ubértasPs 35fartura (com a fartura da vossa casa)openPs 35:9 (πιότης, fatness); abundância is abundántia's; fertilidade of land. 64:12 campi tui replebúntur ubertáte is the other place (grep) — fields: fartura holds there too.

illúsioPs 30 Ps 37escárnio (os meus lombos estão cheios de escárnios)settled (D39)Ps 37:8; 78:4 subsannátio et illúsio the other place (grep). ἐμπαιγμός, mockery. Draft 1 had the cognate ilusões (DRB, MS1932, and L&S's 'eccl.' sense citing this verse): the Latinist marked it MAJOR, the stylist and the blind reader heard fantasies. escárnio is of the family the derídere row gives escarnecer (21:8); derísum (43:14 subsannatiónem et derísum) is the near twin of 78:4 — they never meet in a verse, but the coordinator may want to keep them apart (zombaria is subsannáre's). Option ilusões kept in Ps 37.
lumbiPs 37lombossettled (D39)Ps 37:8 (ψύαι), only place in the psalms (grep). The organ named (rule 5), apart from renes → rins. The blind reader listed lombos unknown and heard the lower back; the Latinist and stylist passed it. Option entranhas (MS1932).
sánitasPs 37saúde (não há saúde na minha carne)workingPs 37:4 = 37:8b, the only places (grep). ἴασις. Heard as bodily health. MS1932 parte sã is the option.
cicatrixPs 37cicatrizsettled (D39)Ps 37:6, only place. The Latin's word (L&S 'a scar'); DRB sores, MS1932 chagas make open wounds. The blind reader found rotting scars puzzling; the Latinist passed it twice. Option chagas.

redargútioPs 37repreensão (e que não tem repreensões na sua boca)settled (D39)Ps 37:15, only place (grep); ἐλεγμοί — the noun of ἐλέγχω, which is árguere → repreender in 37:2, so the echo is kept. The stylist asked réplicas (plainer; refused, option); the blind reader heard 'does not scold others'.

Ps 37:17: the stylist asked grandes coisas ('grandezas suggests quantities or splendours') and the blind reader listed grandezas (as boasts) unknown — kept for 11:4 and 34:26b; if it changes, the three change together.
substántia (a person's being: substántia mea)Ps 38o meu ser (e o meu ser é como nada diante de vós; E o meu ser está junto de vós)settled (D39)Ps 38:6, 38:8 (ὑπόστασις both). a minha substância (DRB) is chemistry or philosophy in Portuguese; MS1932 o meu ser in 38:6 (todos os meus bens in 38:8 is property). Passed the Latinist twice; heard 'my being / existence'. 88:48 quæ mea substántia and 138:15 can follow; 108:11 (property → bens) and 68:3 non est substántia (footing) decide locally (six lines, grep).

Rasteira is the heel-trip and, in Brazil, the figure of a betrayal: image and sense in one word; all three readers heard betrayal. The stylist calls it colloquial; traição (MS1932) is the option.

secúndum elóquium tuum (118:41, 58, 76; 116, 133, 170 to come; 118:154 has propter, 118:169 juxta)Ps 5 Ps 7 Ps 9 Ps 11 Ps 19 Ps 27 Ps 118segundo o que dissestes — settled (D16), replacing segundo o vosso ditoThe clause held on a second blind stylist pass (four of six places passed outright, two mended by word order). History: segundo o vosso dito was tested in Ps 118:33–80 and refused by the stylist in all six places where the singular stands ("a quoted phrase", "bookish", "guardar um dito is to keep a phrase in memory"; he asks for palavra). The blind reader understood it everywhere; the Latinist passed it. It stays in the text under D15 — for the main session to revisit; fall-backs segundo a vossa promessa (fails 118:67) or the merge into palavra (collapses this formula into secúndum verbum tuum, 7 verses away at 118:58 / 65). 118:116: the formula drew no remark from the stylist (his own proposed line keeps it) nor from the blind reader; the Latinist (draft 10, minor) notes that dissestes dates an utterance the noun leaves timeless — D16's named cost 118:133, 170 (the formula) and 118:169 (juxta → conforme o que dissestes; the Latin varies the preposition and Portuguese has the plain pair conforme / segundo): no remark from the stylist on either of two readings, understood by the blind reader twice; the Latinist's minor (past tense) on drafts 13–14, silence on 15–16. 118:154 propter elóquium tuum → pelo que dissestes (not por causa do, which echoed a minha causa in the same verse).

Doublets

13 / 52 · 39:14–18 / 69 · 56:8–12 + 59:7–14 / 107. Whoever translates the second copies the first wherever the Latin is identical, and says so in audit.
